## Document 016: 2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD 
Publishing 
OECD (2026), OECD Economic Surveys: Japan 2026, OECD Publishing

や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

## Document 044: 2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月LPR报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026 年 10 月 LPR 报价保持不变，短期内下调可能性较小 
 王青  冯琳 
2026 年 10 月 20 日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR 报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5 年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

10 月两个期限品种的 LPR 报价保持不变，符合市场预期。主要是 9 月末央行调整完善若
干货币政策工具，主要政策利率保持稳定，这意味着 10 月 LPR 报价的定价基础没有变化。另
外，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附近，报价行也缺乏主动下调 LPR 报价加点的动力。 
我们认为，近期 LPR 报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP 增速回升到 4.5%，以高技术
制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展。在此背景下，总量型货币政策保持定力，货币政
策加大逆周期调节力度优先选择结构性货币政策工具发力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR 报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9 月 29 日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000 亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000 亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000 亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼
市措施出台后，接下来将进入一段政策效果观察期。短期内总量型降息政策跟进的可能性下
降，LPR 报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
再往后看，综合考虑经济金融形势，预计 2027 年央行有望全面降息 10 到 20 个基点
（0.1 到0.2 个百分点），届时将引导 LPR 报价跟进下调。 
10 月 19 日央行宣布，“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，
公开市场隔夜回购利率 将相应成为主要政策利率。”这意味着政策利率“换锚”的大方向已
经确定，未来 LPR 报价的定价基础将由现在的央行 7 天期逆回购利率切换为隔夜逆回购利
率。背后是隔夜政策利率与货币市场隔夜利率（ DR001)之间天然匹配，不存在期限利差，更
有利于提高货币政策传导效率。我们判断，切换时点可能已为时不远，不排除较快落地的可

### Page 2

东方金诚宏观研究

2 
能，届时央行将公开宣布隔夜逆回购操作利率。不过，切换过程带动 LPR 报价下调的可能性
不大；未来 LPR 报价变化，还会主要跟进政策利率水平本身调整。 
10 月 19 日央行还宣布，“推动贷款利率定价基准改革，从比较单一地参考贷款市场报
价利率（LPR）定价向更加多元的定价基准转型。”前期一些省份的银行已经开始发放以 DR
为定价基础的贷款。这意味着贷款利率市场化改革进一步推进，即从原来的单一 LPR 定价基
础，转为“LPR+DR”双锚。LPR 属于机构报价型利率，稳定性较高，而 DR 属于成交型利率，
市场化特征突出。信贷定价转为“ LPR+DR”双锚，意味着后期部分贷款利率市场化定价程度
会更高。这一方面有利于提升货币政策传导效率，加速政策利率向贷款利率传导，另一方面
也能更好满足不同市场主体的融资需求 ——对一些短期流动资金需求大、财务能力强的大型
企业来说，DR 基准贷款更能适配其信贷需求。我们判断，未来 10 年期国债收益率也可能成
为部分贷款的定价基础，背后是一些大型企业有条件在信贷市场和债券市场之间选择融资。
这意味着贷款定价基础多元化还将进一步推进。

Betty.H.Chen@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7213
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 112: 261015_daiwa_TSMC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261015_daiwa_TSMC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: TSMC continued delivering “beat-n-raise” prints for its 
quarterly results and guidance on 15 October. While we raise our forecasts 
to factor in its better topline and pricing trajectory yet muted margin 
expectation on an incremental basis, we see likely lack of near-term 
passion from investors on limited positive surprises from its results call this 
time. That said, we believe AI demand remains strong and sustainable to 
support our Buy (1) call on the stock, though patience may be necessary 
for investors amid the current jittery environment.

What’s the impact: Results intact, guidance mixed. Despite 3Q26 
topline of USD46.7bn topping guidance (USD44.6-45.8bn) at TSMC, its 
3Q26 EPS came in at TWD29.4, only marginally beating our/Bloomberg 
consensus estimates on lower opex and higher non-operating gains from 
long-term investments more than offsetting lower gross margin associated 
with higher depreciation costs on dilutive N2 ramp (table on p.2). Guidance 
calls for higher topline for 4Q26 with midpoint of the range 6% above our 
previous forecast, thanks to continued AI strength as customers continue to 
increase demand. Yet margins at the operating level looked muted without 
further operating leverage being expected by the street, due to the margin-
dilutive N2 ramp. We raise our 2026-28E forecasts by 3-10% to factor in 
the better topline trajectory, plus our view of better pricing power.

Lack of near-term passion? Apart from the upbeat topline but muted 
margin guidance on an incremental basis, we left the results call with 
limited positive surprises this time which may not drive any passion for 
investors in the near term to catalyse the stock. That said, fundamentals 
remain strong since AI demand continues to mushroom from accelerators 
to CPU and other companion chips, as language models transform from 
generative to agentic for AI assistance. TSMC implied upside risk to its 
long-term AI accelerator revenue CAGR of mid-to-high-50% over 2024-29 
and, as flagged in our Asian Foundry report, we saw upside risk to TSMC’s 
pricing power for 2027, given the still meaningful supply constraint where 
management reiterated its strong conviction in this multi-year AI megatrend 
and active capacity build aimed to narrow the supply/demand gap.

What we recommend: We raise our 12M TP for TSMC to TWD3,200 
(previous: TWD3,000), based on our finetuned ROE-adjusted PBR target of 
7.5x one-year forward BVPS forecast (previous: 7.7x). We reiterate our Buy 
(1) call with key downside risks of “silicon inflation” and geopolitical 
overhangs leading to global inflation and AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 11% below the Bloomberg consensus 
likely on our view calling for a soft-landing in the chip cycle.

15 October 2026

Lack of passion despite strong AI?

➢ Another “beat-n-raise” in quarterly prints but likely … 
➢ … short of catalysts on limited positive surprise this time 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD3,200

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan in 1987, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMC) is the world’s largest 
complementary metal oxide semiconductor (CMOS) foundry in revenue terms. TSMC offers foundry 
services such as wafer masking, fabrication, probing, packaging and testing, to a high variety of 
customers including fabless chipmakers and IDMs. Its manufacturing fabs are located in Taiwan, China, 
the US and Singapore.

### Page 5

5

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (2330 TT): 15 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 15 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
TSMC's board of directors (BoD) has 10 members, including 6 independent directors
representing 60% of total directors. The background of TSMC's BoD is diversified, with members
from industries, academia, and law. Also, its board members include representatives from not
only Taiwan but also Europe and the US, bringing their experience in operating international
businesses to the board.
We believe the high percentage of independent directors and a diverse mix of expertise from the
board members strengthen the functionality of its BoD, as evidenced by TSMC's leading position
in the foundry market.
Capital management 1
TSMC pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividend
payout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
As the leader in advanced technology nodes, TSMC has kept expanding its capacity for
advanced nodes; as such, we expect its capital intensity to increase to the level near or above
40% over 2025-26 (vs. its long-term target of mid-30%). However, we believe TSMC will at least
sustain its cash dividend payout in dollar terms over 2025-26E. We see its balance between
investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1
TSMC's sales to related parties and purchases from related parties remain immaterial as a
percentage of total revenue and cost of goods sold in 2026. We view these levels as insignificant
and therefore see limited risk to TSMC's earnings, cash flow generation, or corporate
governance.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
As the leader in the global foundry industry, TSMC approaches supply chain management by
focusing on 2 main strategies: sustainability risk management and local supply chain
optimisation. It requires all its suppliers to comply with its "Code with Ethics and Business
Conduct", and to follow regulations on human rights and conflict-free minerals.
In 2019, 100% of its tier-1 suppliers signed the "Supplier Code of Conduct" and self-assessment
questionnaire on sustainable management. Also, TSMC's raw materials purchased are 100%
Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) conflict-free. It also requires its critical suppliers to
report on the status of sustainability management in their critical upstream supply chains.
Through these measures, we see TSMC reducing its supply chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
TSMC applies internal and external company resources to develop water reclaimation
technology, implements water conservation measures and uses reeclaimed water in the
manufacturing process. For example, on September 19, 2022, TSMC Tainan Science Park
Reclaimed Water Plant started supplying reclaimed water. In 2025, TSMC aims to replace 5% of
water resources with reclaimed water, and continue to cooperate with the government to
complete the 2nd water reclamation plan in Anping, Tainan. In 2030, TSMC targets to replace >
60% of water resources with reclaimed water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
TSMC's waste recycling rate reached 96% in 2022 for 8 years in a row. Also, the percentage of
waste sent to landfills was less than 1% for 13 years in a row in 2022.
TSMC achieved reducing its outsourced unit waste disposal (kg/12" equivalent wafer mask
layer) to 0.99 in 2022. Furthermore, TSMC has set a goal to reduce its outsourced unit waste
disposal per wafer to no more than 0.5 in 2030.
E GHG emissions 1
TSMC has continued its efforts to reduce greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions to drive low-carbon
manufacturing. In 2022, TSMC achieved net zero emissions of direct and indirect (from
purchased energy) GHG Emissions in overseas factories, and 100% use of
carbon-neutral natural gas in Taiwan fabs. It has set a target to achieve Net Zero Emissions by
2050.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部對拓凱未來營運看法轉趨正向 ，主要是考量：(1)占營收比
重最大的自行車業務在 2H26 出現改善，隨著主力客戶新品銷售表
現較佳，帶動訂單增加，預期 2027 年自行車業務有望止跌回溫。
(2)航太、安全帽及其他類業務接單保持強勁，其中航太受惠於航
空座椅需求增加，安全帽則有高階產品及新客戶訂單貢獻，加上精
密塑件業務持續擴張 ， 將有助於優化產品組合 。(3) 2Q26 受子公司
一次性費用及匯率等因素干擾，毛利率與獲利下滑，但隨下半年出
貨規模擴大、產品組合改善，預期 2H26 獲利能力將逐步回升。研
究部預估拓凱 2026 年 EPS 13.52 元(-9%YOY)，2027 年EPS 15.28
元(+13%YOY)。因公司營運重回成長軌道，且產品組合改善有利於
中長期獲利表現，故以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益
比，目標價 183 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要：

1. 主力客戶新車種上市，自行車業務回溫 ： 
拓凱營收約有 50% 來自碳纖維自行車車架與組件，最大核心
客戶為全球知名自行車 品牌-Specialized。雖然公司的自行
車業務在1H26衰退近二成，但受惠主力客戶 年中新品上市並
在終端市場銷售亮眼 ， 客戶對拓凱積極追加車架 、 輪圈與前叉
等複材組件訂單 ， 帶動拓凱近期自行車業務顯著改善 。 在去年
下半年低基期與今年新車拉貨潮帶動下，預計 2026全年自行
車營收減幅將收斂至個位數（研究部估減少8%YoY）。2027年
隨客戶新品需求與庫存去化完畢，預 估自行車業務營收將能
回溫，成長3%YoY。

2. 安全帽、航太及其他類業務展望正向，成長動能強勁： 
自行車以外業務為拓凱營運成長的核心動能 。 其中 ， 拓凱的安
全帽業務近年出貨增加 ， 且產品結構大幅改善 ， 高階產品占比
已達2/3，預計帶動今年安全帽營收大幅增長30%YoY，且因引
進電子智能安全帽新客戶，預期明年該業務營收仍可成長
7%YoY。球拍業務方面，今年出貨暢旺，預期今年球拍營收能
創下歷史新高 ， 全年度球拍營收預計成長18%YoY。 不過 ， 因近
兩年基期拉高，明年看法較謹慎，預計小幅衰退5%YoY。航太
醫療業務方面 ， 航太接單展望樂觀 ， 預期今年該類別營收增長
14%YoY，且1Q27起新增飛機座椅訂單將正式投產 ， 預計明年該
類別營收將再有12%YoY的增速 。 另外 ， 歸類在其他類之子公司

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

新鴻洲部分，受惠自行車煞把手、電子TOF精密塑件等產品出
貨增加 ， 營運動能依舊偏強 ， 預計該業務2026年、2027年營收
分別成長23%、13%YoY。

3. 一次性負面因素淡化，3Q26毛利率與獲利將回升： 
回顧拓凱2Q26財報 ， 受提列子公司一次性費用的衝擊 ， 導致單
季毛利率下滑至31.3%(季減3個百分點，年減6.7個百分點)，
加上業外有逾6千萬元的匯損，拖累季度獲利表現，2Q26稅後
淨利2.67億元(-19%QoQ，-5%YoY)，2Q26EPS下滑至2.94元。然
而 ， 此費用屬於非經常性的短期會計調整 ， 本業高階複材競爭
力並未受損 。 下半年隨自行車業務改善 ， 拓凱3Q26合併營收回
升至27.16億元(+6%QoQ，+12%YoY)。 此外 ， 由於一次性因素淡
化 ， 加上高階安全帽 、 航太及其他類產品出貨增加 ， 產品組合
優化，研究部預估拓凱3Q26毛利率將回升至33.8%，營業利益
5.41億元(+37%QoQ，+13%YoY)，稅後淨利3.55億元(+33%QoQ，
+1%YoY)，3Q26EPS 3.91元，獲利將較第2季明顯改善。

4. 2026年營收近百億元，預估2026年EPS 13.52元： 
受惠於安全帽 、 球拍 、 航太及其他業務成長 ， 抵銷自行車業務
的衰退 ， 預期拓凱全年營收可望接近百億元 ， 研究部預估拓凱
2026年合併營收99.73億元(+8%YoY)。不過，由於2Q26受一次
性費用及匯率等因素干擾，今年度毛利率預估降至33.1%，營
業利益16.91億元(-2%YoY)，稅後淨利12.28億元(-9%YoY)，
EPS 13.52元。

5. 各業務成長帶動獲利回升，預估2027年EPS 15.28元： 
展望2027年 ， 隨自行車業務恢復成長 ， 航太及安全帽新訂單持
續貢獻，以及其他類業務穩健擴張，研究部預估 2027年營收
104.34億元(+5%YoY)， 毛利率回升至33.8%， 營業利益18.50億
元(+9%YoY)，稅後淨利13.88億元(+13%YoY)，EPS 15.28元。

6. 越南產能持續擴充，並積極佈局新應用商機 ： 
因應客戶分散生產基地與供應鏈調整需求，拓凱持續擴充越
南複合材料產品線 ， 目前除自行車前叉 、 車架等零組件外 ， 亦
逐步增加航空座椅零組件的生產，未來航太板材及球拍等產
品亦有機會導入越南製造 。 公司也已著手規劃越南二期廠房 ，
強化海外生產彈性 。 此外 ， 中科后里新廠除作為營運總部及研
發據點外 ， 也將支援新業務開發 。 初期鎖定人形機器人關鍵的
腰部與膝蓋複材結構件與客戶進行共同開發，同時也積極切
入農業與商用無人機 、 低軌衛星等先端應用 。 子公司新鴻洲方

面 ， 則運用精密射出及模具技術 ， 計畫開拓半導體與人形機器

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com 
       廖婉婷 TINA_LIAO@uni-psg.com

臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元 ， 改寫單月新高，3Q26 營收 646.24 
億元，優於研究部原估的 607.09 億元，主因 AI 相關業務出貨
優於預期 ， 加上手機客戶進入新品旺季 ， 研究部據此上修 3Q26 稅
後 EPS 至 3.84 元（原估 3.47 元）。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺
季延續， 加上 GPU 與 ASIC 新一代主板開始放量 ，營收與獲利將
續創新高 。2027 年伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成
為主流， 營收可望各再增逾百億元；IC 載板亦受惠供給持續吃緊
及新產能陸續開出 ， 帶動 AI 相關營收占比突破三成 。 綜上所述 ，
研究部上修臻鼎 2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 至 12.11 元（+99.6% 
YoY，原估 11.81 元）及 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，原估 22.91 
元）。基於臻鼎產品橫跨軟板、類載板、HDI、HLC 與 IC 載板，
可為客戶提供一站式解決方案 ， 並提前掌握關鍵材料與設備產能 ，
在高階 PCB 供給吃緊下擴產得以大量開出搶奪市占，研究部維
持以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎及 28 倍本益比，目標價上調至 
730 元，維持買進投資評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 9 月營收改寫新高，上修 2H26 獲利預估 
臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元（+27.5% MoM、+36.1% YoY），改寫
單月新高 ， 其中伺服器 、 光模塊與 IC 載板合計營收年增逾三倍 ，
行動通訊亦呈雙位數月增 與年增 。3Q26 營收 646.24 億元
（+33.4% QoQ、+36.4% YoY），達研究部原估的 106.4%，明顯優
於預期 ， 主因 GPU 新一代主板進入量產 、1.6T 光模塊出貨放大 ，
加上手機客戶進入新品旺季。考量 AI 相關產品占比提升，研究
部預估 3Q26 毛利率為 25.5%（+2.4ppt QoQ、+3.5ppt YoY，原
估 24.7%），稅後 EPS 上修至 3.84 元（+81.9% QoQ 、+81.9% 
YoY，原估 3.47 元） 。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺季延續，加上 GPU 
與 ASIC 新一代主板放量，研究部預估 4Q26 營收 736.13 億元
（+13.9% QoQ、+29.4% YoY），稅後 EPS 4.75 元（+23.8% QoQ、
+70.4% YoY，原估 4.86 元），營收與獲利續創新高。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. AI 伺服器主板成為最主要成長動能 
AI 伺服器主板隨 GPU 與 ASIC 平台升級，晶片封裝尺寸、I/O 
數與傳輸速率同步提升，主板須在有限面積內完成更多高速訊號
的扇出與繞線。 傳統 HLC 以一次壓合搭配機械鑽孔與背鑽 ，層間
連接密度已近極限；高階 HDI 則以多次壓合與雷射盲孔逐層堆
疊，線路更細、訊號路徑更短，可降低高速傳輸損耗，帶動 GPU 
平台 OAM 等主板設計由 HLC 轉向 HDI。HDI 須投入大量雷射鑽
孔機與多次壓合產能，在設備與材料交期拉長下，全球高階  AI 
PCB 產能缺口約 15–20%， 訂單已排至 2028 年，2027–2028 年
產品報價亦將持續上調。臻鼎提早取得全球逾五成高階雷射鑽孔
機產能 ， 在建 22 座新廠中半數投入 HDI、 伺服器與光模塊產品 ，
在同業擴產受限下得以持續承接訂單，帶動伺服器 HDI 比重由 
1H26 的約五成於 2H26 明顯提升；繼 ASIC 專案今年大量出貨
後，GPU 平台新一代主板亦自 4Q26 起放量。研究部預估臻鼎伺
服器營收 2026、2027 年各增加逾 100 億元 ， 為未來兩年最主要
成長動能。

3. 臻鼎可望躍居 1.6T 光模塊 PCB 龍頭 
AI 伺服器傳輸速率由 800G 升級至 1.6T，單通道速率倍增使線
路須更細 、 阻抗控制更精準 ， 傳統減成法已難以達到 ， 光模塊 PCB 
因此改採 mSAP 製程。臻鼎過去為手機類載板主要供應商，具備 
mSAP 大量生產經驗 ； 在高階設備交期拉長 、 關鍵材料供給吃緊下 ，
臻鼎已掌握市場上多數高階設備產能並持續擴充，同時提前鎖定
上游材料，得以在淮安與泰國同步擴充光模塊產能，研究部推估
臻鼎可望在 2027 年 1.6T 世代成為全球光模塊 PCB 龍頭。隨 
1.6T 出貨占比由 2026 年的約五成提升至 2027 年逾七成，研
究部預估臻鼎光模塊營收由 2025 年約 9 億元躍升至 2026 年
逾 100 億元，2027 年再增至逾 250 億元。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. ABF 與 BT 同步吃緊，新產能與轉單推升載板營收 
AI 晶片封裝面積與層數持續放大，ABF 載板用量與製程難度同
步提升，惟新廠建置期長、上游材料吃緊，供需缺口持續擴大；
同業亦將部分 BT 產能轉往 ABF，使 BT 載板同步出現缺貨，兩
者報價自下半年起持續上漲。臻鼎 ABF 載板技術規格已達最大 
158×158mm、 最高 28 層 ， 深圳一廠 1Q26 轉盈並將於年底滿載 ，
二廠 1Q27 開始貢獻，第二園區 2028 年起約每年開出一座新
廠，且新產能幾乎全由國際客戶預先包下，高雄廠亦已量產先進
封裝相關載板。BT 載板雖無新建廠計畫， 但近期承接同業轉單並
調漲報價 ， 加上產品與客戶組合持續優化 ， 可望維持雙位數成長 。
隨 ABF 產能接續開出，研究部預估 ABF 占載板營收比重將由 
2025 年的 35% 提升至 2027 年約七成，IC 載板營收 2026、
2027 年分別成長 99.2% 及 89.0%。

5. 高階新產能陸續開出，產品組合優化推升毛利率 
臻鼎 2026 年資本支出逾 800 億元，2027 年維持相當水準，廠
房數將由目前的 28 座擴增至 2030 年底的 59 座。新產能自 
2027 年起陸續開出：淮安 6 座新廠於 2026 年底完工後進入裝
機，HD 園區預計 3M27 投產、2Q27 大批量產；泰國新廠於 4Q26 
起試產，1H27 進入小量生產 ； 深圳第三園區與載板第二園區則預
計自 2028 年起接棒 （表二） 。新廠初期稼動率與良率仍在爬坡，
加上 2027 年折舊費用預計較 2026 年再增加 20% 至 30%，短
期將稀釋毛利率；惟新增產能以 AI 伺服器、光模塊與 IC 載板
為主，隨 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 2027 
年突破三成，研究部預估臻鼎毛利率將由 2026 年的 24.5% 提
升至 2027 年的 29.3%。

6. 上修 2026、2027 年獲利預估 
2026 年 AI 相關業務為主要成長動能 ，研究部預估伺服器 、光模
塊與其他營收成長 269.0%，IC 載板成長 99.2%，行動通訊受惠
摺疊機新品成長 6.9%，電腦消費則與 2025 年持平，AI 相關營
收占比將由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 25.9%。研究部預估 2026 
年營收 2,273.96 億元（+24.6% YoY，原估 2,267.70 億元），
毛利率由 24.1% 上修至 24.5%（+4.7ppt YoY ），稅後 EPS 由 
11.81 元上修至 12.11 元（+99.6% YoY）。 
展望 2027 年，伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成為
主流，營收合計成長逾一倍，IC 載板受惠 AI 客戶訂單放量、
ABF 比重提升及報價調漲，成長 89.0%，行動通訊與電腦消費則
分別維持中、低個位數成長。在高毛利產品占比提升、費用率隨
營收規模擴大回落，抵銷新廠折舊增加下，研究部預估 2027 年
營收 3,158.99 億元（+38.9% YoY，原估 3,083.20 億元），毛
利率提升至 29.3%（+4.8ppt YoY，原估 28.1%） ，營益率提升至 
17.9%（原估 14.8%） ， 稅後 EPS 上修至 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，
原估 22.91 元）。

## Document 209: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가드디어 엔진 증설, 저평_dc1151a4

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가드디어 엔진 증설, 저평_dc1151a4.pdf

### Page 1

2026.09.11  
HD 현대중공업 (329180)

드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가  Flash Note 
[조선/기계/방산] 변용진 2122-9181 yongjin.byun@imfnsec.com

Check Point  Buy (Maintain) 
HD현대중공업 중형엔진 및 SMR 공장 증설 결정  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

## Document 224: AIを活用する消費者に企業はどう向き合うか～生成AI利用者の３割半ばが家族・友人より先にAIへ相談

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIを活用する消費者に企業はどう向き合うか～生成AI利用者の３割半ばが家族・友人より先にAIへ相談.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1――はじめに ～ AIとの相談で生まれた消費者の期待を、企業は店頭でどう受け止めるか

1｜はじめに ～ AIが消費者の意思決定に与える影響とリスクを考える 
内閣府消費者委員会の「人工知能（AI）技術の利用と消費者問題に関する専門調査会」では、AIが
消費者の意思決定に与える影響とリスクが検討されている。事務局の検討状況案によれば、個人に合
わせたAIの働きかけによって、消費者自身が影響に気づかないまま判断し、問題があっても相談や救
済につながりにくい可能性が、課題とされている。

たとえば、商品選びでも、AIとの相談によりニーズが整理される一方、AIから提供される情報の偏
りや見落としに気づかず、購入後に予想外の費用や手間を知る場合が考えられる。 
本稿では、専門調査会のこうした問題意識を踏まえ、消費者の購入前のAIとの相談、店舗やサイト
での説明、使用後の経験のつながりに注目し、商品の使い方や必要な手間について、企業の説明や対
応で何を補えるか、使用後の経験をどのような改善に活かせるかを考えていく。

2｜生成 AI利用者の35.0％が、家族・友人より先にAIへ相談する 
総務省「令和8年版情報通信白書」によれば、日本の生成AI利用経験は、20歳以上で2024年度調
査の 26.7％から2025年度調査の52.2％へ上昇した。1また、消費者庁「令和 8年版消費者白書」2で
は、商品・サービス購入を支援するAIを、検索や商品の比較に使う実態も示されている。AIとの事
前相談を経て店舗やサイトを訪れる消費者は今後、一層増えていくことが予想される。 
また、内閣府による生成 AI利用者の利用実態調査の結果3によれば、生成 AI利用者のうち 35.0％

1 総務省. (2026). 令和 8年版情報通信白書（第Ⅰ部第1章第 1節、図表Ⅰ-1-1-1）. 白書の数値は生成AI全般の利用経験を示している。 
2 消費者庁. (2026). 令和 8年版消費者白書（第Ⅰ部第 2章第 2節、図表Ⅰ-2-2-16）. 
3 内閣府消費者委員会事務局. (2026). 生成 AI利用者の利用実態に関するアンケート結果。2026年 2月 16～18日実施／インターネット調
査／日本在住10歳以上の生成AI利用者1442人が対象。男女×7年代に各103人を割り付け（人口構成に比例した標本ではない） 
2026-10-15

3｜AIとの事前相談は、自分が欲しい機種の整理に繋がるのか 
「令和8年版情報通信白書」でも、 「日常の生活に伴う購入を検討・判断するとき」に何かしらの形
でAIを利用する人は、全体の37.4％と４割近くにものぼる。4 
消費者による商品購入時のAIへの相談場面としては、商品を選ぶときに、AIに予算や使う場所を
伝えて、おすすめを聞いたり、候補を比べたりする、といった使い方が考えられる。 
たとえば、この具体的なケースとして、家電の「掃除機」の買い替えをイメージしてみたい。 
ある消費者が自宅の部屋の広さなど購入要件としてAIに伝えるうちに、 「吸引力も気になるが、ゴ
ミ捨てやフィルター清掃などの手入れを減らしたい」と新たな希望がはっきりしてくる場合がある。 
その過程で、そのような購入要件や候補機種を整理できれば、その後で店舗を訪問したときに店員
に対して相談しやすくなるだろう。 
その一方で、 その消費者が候補とした掃除機について、 「ゴミを捨てる時など、 使用時の手入れが楽」
というAIからの事前説明を受けて、実際には手入れが必要であるにもかかわらず、 「ほとんど手入れ
が要らない」と受け止めていたらどうだろうか。 
購入後に、使って初めて必要な作業の手間を知り、戸惑うことがあるかもしれない。無論、購入前
に、その作業を正確に知っていれば、別の機種を選んだ可能性もありうる。

2――消費者が商品を選ぶ理由は、相談の中でも形づくられる

4 総務省. (2026). 令和 8年版情報通信白書（第Ⅰ部第 1章第 1節、図表Ⅰ-1-1-12） 「日常の生活に伴う購入を検討・判断するとき」の
AI利用の数値。なおAIのみは2.9％、主にAIは 10.1％、主に他の方法でAIを補助的に利用は24.4％（合計37.4％）となる。 
資料：　内閣府消費者委員会事務局（2026）計は表示した2区分の合計。

### Page 3

しかし、問題は、AIとの対話で自分が重視する点が整理され、特定の商品やサービスへの購入意向
が高まったとしても、その商品が本当に自分の暮らしに適しているとは限らないことである。 
また、消費者に対するAIの影響について、冒頭で紹介した専門調査会の事務局資料でも、たとえば
AIの質問や話し方が、消費者の好みや希望の形成に影響する可能性として指摘されている。7 
また、消費者行動のある関連研究によれば、消費者の商品について重視する点が、購入・使用前は
「機能の豊富さ」 、使用後には「使いやすさ」という様に変化する傾向が見られると言う。8

これを掃除機の例で言えば、AIと「性能面」を中心に相談して商品を選んだ後、実際の使用時には、
事前相談で重視していなかった持ち運びや手入れが気になる、といったケースが考えられる。

3――利用実態の数字のみならず、購入まで・利用後までの経過を見る

1｜「生成 AIの回答をそのまま受け入れてしまうことが多い」が34.3％ 
それでは、消費者の視点からは、AIの回答はどのように受け止められているのだろうか。 
前掲の専門調査会で紹介された研究によれば、AIからの説明を、利用者が重視する点に合わせて提
供すると、ある予測課題で説明への満足度などが高まったことが示されている。9 
また、別の関連する研究では、AIが回答に理由を添えると、正しい回答だけでなく、誤った回答も

品理解の研究ではな点に注意。

### Page 4

また先掲の内閣府の調査によれば、生成AI利用者のうち、AIの回答をそのまま受け入れてしまう
ことが多いという回答が34.3％と全体の約１/３、そして、AIの回答に納得できなくても自分の意見
を変えることがあるという回答が20.5％であった。 
この結果は、商品やサービスの購入に限らず、日常生活全般での生成AI利用について尋ねたもので
あり、回答を受け入れたり意見を変えたりした理由、 その結果が本人に役立ったかまでは分からない。 
ただし、AIの回答をそのまま受け入れたり、納得できなくても自分の意見を変えたりする利用者が
一定数いることは伺える結果である。

 生成
 AI
利用者の回答受容・意見変更に関する実感

2｜相談 AIの対話の自然さや人間らしさも高まりつつある  
さらに、昨今の生成AIを用いた相談サービスでは、対話の自然さや人間らしさも高まりつつある。 
関連する研究では、AIの人間らしい反応が利用者の信頼を高め得ることや、AIによる説明や説得
が、内容の正誤とは必ずしも一致しない形で利用者の信頼に影響する可能性も指摘されている。11 
これは、AIによる説明が分かりやすく、もっともらしく感じられていれば、その内容が誤っていて
も、 消費者が誤りに気づかないまま、 誤った理解に基づき選択する可能性があることを示唆している。 
今後、相談AIの対人性が高まるほど、その利用促進と併せて、利用者がAIに寄せる信頼が、AIの
実際の能力や回答の確かさに適切に対応しているかも確認する必要があると言えるだろう。

10 Kim, S. S. Y ., Vaughan, J. W., Liao, Q. V ., Lombrozo, T., & Russakovsky, O. (2025). Fostering appropriate reliance on large language 
models: The role of explanations, sources, and inconsistencies. In Proceedings of the 2025 CHI Conference on Human Factors in 
Computing Systems. Association for Computing Machinery . 
11 Tan, L., Li, Z., Huang, Y., Liu, J., Ni, M., Wang, S., Che, J., & Chen, K. (2026). DHCBT: A digital human psychological counseling 
system with a CBT multi-agent framework. Scientific Reports. Advance online publication. 
人間らしい相談インターフェースのシステムが、テキスト型システムより、利用者に「相手がそこにいる」という感覚を強く与え、継続利
用したいという評価を高めた。 
  資料：　内閣府消費者委員会事務局（2026）計は表示した2区分の合計。

### Page 5

ここまで見てきたように、消費者の期待と、製品・サービスの実際の仕様や使い方に違いがあると、
必要な情報を十分に知らないまま購入し、想定した用途に使えなかったり、予想外の費用や負担が生
じたりする可能性がある。そして、この問題は、消費者が十分な情報を得て商品を選べたかという、
消費者支援上の課題にもつながっていく。 
さらに、企業にとっても、場合によっては満足度やNPS（Net Promoter Score）などの顧客評価が
下がるリスクになり得る。

この点を、企業の店頭やサイト運営者の視点から見ると、AIとの事前相談を経た顧客のニーズや期
待を、サイトでの説明や店頭での接客において、どのように受け止めるかという問いにもつながる。 
下表（表1）は、AIとの相談を経た消費者に生じ得る情報の偏りや見落としについて、関連研究を
踏まえ、確認の着眼点として整理したものである。

表 
 1 
 AI
との事前相談を経た商品選びで、確かめたい偏り・見落とし

このように、消費者がAIとの事前相談を経たとしても、情報の偏りや見落としが残る場合がある。 
しかも、企業はその相談内容を十分に把握できるとは限らない。仮に、「企業が提供する AI」を通
じて生まれた期待であれば、設計や権限によっては、相談内容や履歴を参照し、説明を点検する手掛
かりを得られるだろう。一方、顧客が個人で使うChatGPTなどの汎用的なAIサービスでは、その相談
内容を企業が十分に確認できるとは限らない。 
また、AIとの事前相談内容を「個人的なもの」と感じて、事業者への開示に抵抗感を示す消費者も
いると思われる。AIとの事前相談の内容を詳しく聞くことにこだわらず、本人がストレスを感じるこ
となく話せる範囲の情報をもとに、必要な対応を検討していく視点も必要であろう。

着眼点 
  偏り・見落としの可能性
おすすめの前提
 AIに伝えた用途や予算、使う環境が十分でなく、
おすすめが本人の実情に合っていない。
比較する候補
 AIが薦めた商品だけを検討し、自分に合うほかの選択肢が
比較から抜けている。
比較する基準
 価格や性能を中心に比べ、重さ、収納、手入れなど、
 暮らしに重要な点を見落としている。
情報の正確さ
 説明に納得していても、商品の仕様や注意点に誤りや
 抜けがないかを確かめていない。
日常での使い方
 機能や仕様の違いは分かっていても、使う際の作業や手間、
 継続してかかる費用まで想像できていない。
　出典：Häubl & Trifts（2000）、Häubl & Murray（2003）、Thompson et al.（2005）、Kim et al.（2025）を踏まえ
 　　　　 ニッセイ基礎研究所で整理。

### Page 6

その前提に立ったとき、企業はどのような打ち手が考えられるのだろうか。 
まず、消費者自身に、AIとの事前相談を通して重視した点を振り返り、自分の暮らしに大切な点が
比較に含まれていたかを、自ら確かめる、気づける機会を提供していく方法が考えられる。 
例えば、掃除機を例とすれば、機種を価格や吸引力で選んだと話す消費者に対して、本体の重さ、
収納時に必要なスペース、ゴミ捨てやフィルター清掃などについても比較したかを、必要に応じて具
体的に尋ねるような方法で想定される。 
さらに、期待と実際に違いがありそうな点を、図や動画、具体的な使用場面で説明することも、消
費者自身が違いに気づくきっかけになり得る。掃除機なら、ゴミ捨てやフィルター清掃を実演して見
せることで、 「想像より手間がかかる」と分かる場合があるだろう。 
これらはいずれも、従来の顧客対応の延長上で対処できるアプローチでもあるとも言える。

もう１つは、企業でよく行われている消費者（顧客）に対する満足度やNPS（Net Promoter Score）
といった顧客調査の活用する方法も考えられる。 
店舗やサイトで説明を受ける直前（AIとの事前相談をしていれば、その後）に、その商品をどのよ
うに使えると思っていたか（事前期待） 、購入時の店頭やサイトの説明で分かったこと（理解） 、使用
後に感じた期待との違い（事前期待の実態の差についての気づき）を聞く。そして、事前のAI相談に
ついては、その有無のみを併せて聞く。 
これにより、事前のAI相談が期待にどう影響し、期待をどう形成したかまでは分からないものの、
企業にとって商品の改善が必要なのか、店頭やサイトでの説明や相談を補う必要があるのか、を考え
やすくなる。消費者支援の施策面でも、消費者にとってどのような場面で情報が不足しがちなのか、
既存の説明や相談の過程のどこを見直せるかを知る材料にはなる。

こうしたアプローチは、購入時・使用時への対応を中心としてきた従来の顧客へのアプローチに対
して、自社との接触前に行われる「消費者とAIとの事前相談」までをスコープに組み入れ、相談、購
入時の説明、使用後の経験を一体として捉える対応とも言えるだろう。

本稿では、消費者によるAIへの事前相談を出発点に、店舗やサイトでの説明、使用後の経験を一体
として考え、今後の消費者への支援に活かすアプローチに着目してきた。 
次稿では生命保険の調査と聞き取りを取り上げ、既存の説明や意向確認との関係から、この課題を
より具体化して考えていく。

本資料記載のデータは各種の情報源から入手・加工したものであり、その正確性と完全性を保証するものではありません。 
また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

## Document 324: KGI Weekly Kickstart (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/KGI Weekly Kickstart (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: KGI (凱基證券)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

► 伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝組織持續攻擊沙烏
地阿 拉 伯 和荷 姆 茲 海峽 船 隻，美 期 中 選舉
前，地緣政治風險不減 ，添股市賣壓 。中國
長鑫存儲在新型晶片研發取得進展 ，衝擊半
導體類股 ，美光與三星 、海力士記憶體廠即
使仍供不應求 ，亦遭市場拋售 ，南韓股指週
跌幅逾 18% 。 網 通 晶 片 大 廠 博 通 執 行 長 表

示：資金是 AI建設擴張面臨的最大瓶頸 ，光
通訊股領跌走弱，科技、半導體股重挫。
► 美股第三季財報登場 ，花旗、高盛本季淨利
優於預期 ，但富國銀行淨利息收入失色 ，摩
根大通自 2023年4Q以來 ，首次獲利不如預
期，美銀亦不佳 。艾斯摩爾上修全年營收展
望，透露部分客戶已洽詢2029年至2030年設
備訂單，顯示晶片擴產需求仍強 。台積電3Q
毛利率達66.4%較前月略減，因2奈米快速放
量與海外廠產能利用率造成稀釋 ，但今年資
本支出財測維持600到640億美元不變，反映
代理AI客戶需求強勁 ，預期2026年至2028年
資本支出將較過去三年進一步顯著提高。

► 美國 9 月CPI 、PPI 皆上升 ， 且 超 過 市 場 預
期，9月PPI月增率為 1.3%，創2022年來最
大漲幅，高於市場預期的月增 0.5%，年增率
達6.3%，為2022年12月來最大漲幅 ，顯示
通膨壓力正從能源擴大至其他領域 。通膨僵
固性使市場預期高利率將維持更久時間 ，美
國公債殖利率一度走高 ，但隨股市重挫 、避
險資金轉入 ，公債止跌回升 。體質較弱的企
業債出現警訊 ，CoreWeave債券殖利率大幅
攀升 ， 2032 年 到 期 債 券 殖 利 率 一 度 升 至
15%，反應公司舉債擴建資料中心 ，市場擔
憂其債 務規 模 、 利 息負擔 及未來 再融 資能
力，除違約風險上升 ，過高成本侵蝕企業獲
利、股債皆挫 ，顯示目前即使是 AI、科技信
用債表現也開始出現分化。
► 中東地緣風險推升油價上漲 ，儘管川普宣布

將暫時放寬對俄羅斯柴油出口的限制 ，西德
州油價仍處於每桶 98美元高位 ，推升全球通
膨預期 。虛擬資產比特幣 、以太幣隨風險性
資產同步下挫。

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至 3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由 2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率 0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因 ，
貢獻整體 CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導 ，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性 ，需求面
通膨略有提高 ，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在 4%以上維持更久 。受
到油價高盤 、通膨僵固 ，與財政惡化 、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響 ，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中 ， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈 ，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大 、
歐元走弱 ，美元轉強 ，商品、黃金承壓 。法
國政治 不確定性讓 歐股修正 、法股最疲 ，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高 ，增加企業借貸成本

與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。
熱門議題
熱門議題

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，Goldman Sachs，凱基證券整理，註：右上圖之大型雲端服務供應商(CSP)企業為Amazon、Alphabet、Microsoft、Oracle
► 美國第三季財報季展開 ，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現 。根據FactSet數據
顯示 ，標普500指數第三季獲利 成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐 ，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI 需求帶動 ， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股 ，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力 。高盛指出 ，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普 500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長 ，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響 ，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間 ，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面 。隨財報
數據陸續公布 ，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現 ，並維持正向財測 ，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價 ，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐
熱門議題
美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張
熱門議題財報展望

投資焦點
► 醫療保健 、醫療服務 與生物技術等行業 ，在
景氣不好時 ，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高 ，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天 ，股市波動風險上升 ，觀察
過去當 VIX恐慌指數上揚時 ，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯 ，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率 、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑 ，部分公司估值偏低 。當
臨床數據改善 、或導入AI篩選資料與 藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值 。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力 ，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線 ，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值 ，帶動整體產業估值回升 。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場 需求，
創新藥物 (如瘦瘦針 )讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場 2030年銷售額預估上調 15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。

醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健
市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長
投資焦點
醫療生技

### Page 8

8
內部參考文件請勿外流
► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響 ，由於商業雇主
贊助保險 (ESI)和平價醫療法案 (ACA)的自付
額增加 ，以及最高自付費用要求 ，較去年同
期提高 ，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數 、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善 。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健 ，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費 ，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限 )來擴大利潤空間 ，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合 ，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險 (ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去 20 年來的最高水準 。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加 ，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑 。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

投資焦點
近3個月漲幅和預估獲利呈正成長，醫療生技股佳
醫療保健和生技預估獲利明年明顯成長，有利股價續揚
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基投顧，凱基證券整理
預估今明年產業獲利差異，醫療保健和生技成長最大

### Page 9

9
內部參考文件請勿外流
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境使 短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金

融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

WEEKLY AGGREGATES
ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_874
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

Sources: ANZ, Westpac
Despite risk, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• Ongoing Middle East tensions are increasing volatility and raising energy, input
and shipping costs, while broadly supporting prices for agricultural products.

Impacts vary depending on idiosyncratic supply and demand conditions.
• Risks are exacerbated by the impact of a now confirmed super-sized El Niño,
which could slice off up to 1ppt of New Zealand’s GDP should on-farm
production of both milk and meat contract significantly.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg, with milk powder prices holding up better than we expected. El Niño
presents a clear upside risk to this, as it does not appear to have been reflected

in GDT auction prices to date.
• Meanwhile average New Zealand beef export prices should remain elevated over
the coming year, as lean trimming prices reverse recent declines. Sheepmeat
prices are set to ease on rising Australian supply, but remain at elevated levels.
• Kiwifruit and apple export prices are expected to remain elevated, supported
by strong demand for premium varieties, although increasing supply is likely to
keep prices largely flat rather than drive further gains.

• Export prices for New Zealand logs are set to remain broadly stable to slightly
firmer, supported by lower log supply and balanced Chinese inventories.

Source: Macrobond, Stats NZ, Westpac
%yr Index
La Niña
El Niño
Farming is well placed to navigate impacts of a strong El Niño.
• Scientists have now confirmed that the current El Niño event will be the

strongest on record. Temperatures in the Pacific Ocean are now 3.05C above the
average, exceeding the record set in 2015.
• A supersized El Niño increases the probability of drier weather in eastern regions
of New Zealand in coming months, with wetter conditions likelier in the west and
southern areas of the country.
• The potential impact on GDP could be significant. Our previous research has
shown a severe drought could slice off as much as 1ppt from economic growth.

• Much of that is will reflect lower agriculture production, particularly in dairy,
sheep and beef farms. Increased on-farm costs and processing disruptions are
also likely.
• A 1% loss in GDP is likely an upper end estimate of the El Niño impact. A key
consideration is soil moisture levels are currently high, implying some resilience
to future drier conditions.
• Farming practices have become more resilient to prolonged dry periods.
Irrigation is more widely used than in past dry periods and snowmelt levels

appear robust, which should be of particular benefit to Canterbury farms.

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Farm input costs are on the rise reflecting two key drivers: fuel and
fertiliser costs.
• Diesel and petrol prices have increased by about 50% compared to the same
period in 2025. While average prices fell in the September quarter, they closed

the quarter at an elevated level as tensions between the US and Iran increased
and broadened to encompass Saudi Arabia and the Yemeni Houthis.
• Fertiliser prices are also significantly higher. While the fertiliser market has
stabilised after a period of extreme volatility earlier this year, prices remain
highly sensitive to developments in the Middle East.
• Nitrogen (urea) prices have fallen substantially from a mid-year spike, with an
pick up in Chinese exports now boosting supply. These prices though remain

vulnerable to supply disruptions.
• By contrast, phosphate and other sulphur-based fertiliser prices are tracking
higher and remain at elevated levels, as supply constraints, trade restrictions
and disruptions to shipping offset the impact of softening demand in some
regions of the world.

Sources: Fonterra, Stats NZ, Westpac Westpac
forecasts
Farmgate milk price forecast revised up on strong protein demand.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg. This is above Fonterra’s forecast midpoint of $9.50/kg (range of $8.50-

10.50), but lower than futures market pricing of just over $10/kg.
• We have also opened our forecast for the 2027/28 season at $9.50/kg. Note that
there is a wide margin of uncertainty around the forecast at this stage, and we
will review as the next season approaches.
• Dairy prices at auction have generally held up better than our expectations,
although the performance has varied across products. Trends in global demand
are shifting away from fats and towards proteins, which has boosted demand

and prices for skim milk powder in particular.
• Our forecasts do not include an assumption about the impact of El Niño, which
presents a clear upside risk to prices and a downside risk to volumes. While the
futures market is pricing a strong lift in milk powder prices over the New Zealand
summer, there has been no apparent urgency to buy at the GDT auction.
Fonterra is forecasting a 2% lift in milk volumes over the rest of this season, on
top of what was already a strong increase last season.

• Global milk production also remains ample, although it has slowed in Europe
in recent months. El Niño is less likely to affect conditions among the major
producers in the Northern Hemisphere.

Export beef prices to remain elevated over the next year.
• Beef export prices remain well supported but have softened in recent
months. The weakness has been concentrated in lean beef trimming prices,

which have eased since April as high US prices attracted additional imports,
particularly from South America, while tariff-free quotas temporarily boosted
product availability.
• In contrast, premium beef prices have remained firm, supported by tight global
supplies of high-quality beef, constrained US cattle numbers, and resilient
demand in higher-end retail and foodservice channels.
• We expect that lean trimming prices will reverse recent declines in coming

months as temporary increases in supply begin to fade and underlying
shortages, including the historically small US beef herd, re-emerge.
• In 2027, lean trimming prices should remain elevated but broadly flat as
strong US demand and a small US cattle herd are largely offset by rising global
production. From 2028 onwards, expanding global beef production gradually
eases market conditions, putting pressure on prices.
• Meanwhile, favourable supply/demand fundamentals to support premium beef

prices in 2027, with expanding global production to weigh on prices from 2028.
• Overall, beef export prices are likely to remain high, although both nominal and
real prices are expected to moderate over the medium term.

Lamb export prices to remain firm, but off recent highs.
• NZ sheepmeat export prices remain near record highs, supported by
constrained global supply and resilient demand from premium markets in

Europe, the UK and North America. Export values rose strongly through 2025/26
despite only modest growth in export volumes, highlighting the strength of
underlying prices.
• Global supply remains tight due to declining sheep flocks and lower slaughter
numbers in New Zealand, the UK and parts of Europe. However, rising Australian
production and exports are expected to gradually lift global supply and increase
competition in key export markets, limiting further price gains.

• Meanwhile, stronger demand from the UK, EU and North America has improved
export returns and reduced New Zealand's reliance on China, where economic
weakness has made consumers more price sensitive and encouraged a shift
towards cheaper proteins and cuts.
• The outlook remains positive but more balanced. Tight supply should keep
prices above historical averages over the coming year, although rising Australian
exports, softer demand growth, affordability pressures, signs of buyer

resistance and (eventually) a stronger NZ dollar could see prices ease modestly
from current elevated levels into 2027.

Capacity is catching up with demand, limiting further price gains.
• NZ kiwifruit export prices have pushed to record highs, supported by strong
demand in North America, Europe, Japan and Korea, as well as continued

consumer preference for premium varieties such as SunGold.
• Export prices continue to edge higher even as a record crop and larger fruit
volumes are helping to reduce market tightness. Zespri notes increased
competition from summer fruit, pricing pressure in parts of Asia and a larger-
than-expected crop.
• Kiwifruit export prices are set to remain high but mostly flat over the next
year, as strong demand for premium varieties such as SunGold and RubyRed is

increasingly offset by rising production and price pressures in Asia.
• Apple export prices are also at record highs, supported by strong demand in
Asia, North America and India, alongside a continued shift towards higher-value
cultivars and premium varieties.
• These factors should keep export prices elevated over the next 12 months,
supporting grower revenues and profitability.
• However, further gains could be limited. Rising New Zealand production,

recovering supplies from key growing regions and larger global apple crops are
expected to increase supply availability, reducing market tightness and keeping
prices broadly stable.

Source: AgriHQ
Index IndexLog prices look moribund.
• China remains the dominant market for New Zealand logs, with prices highly
sensitive to construction activity, sawmill demand and daily log offtake. In that
regard, weak housing and furniture demand continue to weigh on prices.

• Chinese softwood log inventories are estimated at around 2.2 to 2.5m m³,
equivalent to roughly 40 to 45 days of inventory cover at current offtake rates.
This suggests inventory levels are broadly balanced and exerting little pressure
on log prices.
• At the same time, reduced New Zealand log supply, including lower harvesting
volumes and the wind-down of salvage logging, is providing some support to
prices. Freight costs also remain an important driver of at-wharf-gate returns,

with currently high shipping costs crimping returns to forest owners.
• NZ log export prices are expected to remain broadly stable to modestly firmer
over the next 12 months, supported by lower domestic supply, balanced Chinese
inventory levels and tentative signs of stabilisation in log demand.
• However, any recovery is likely to be gradual, with weakness in China's property
sector, uncertainty around shipping costs and softer global growth continuing to

constrain demand and cap upside potential.

## Document 342: NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Higher oil and refined fuels prices, a lower
exchange rate, higher long term interest rates
and weak house price trends justify a review of
our 2026 forecasts.
• We continue to see CPI inflation falling to 3.7%
y/y in the September quarter, but now think
inflation will rise to 4.2% in the December
quarter. Higher global fuel prices and a weaker
New Zealand dollar are driving these trends.
• Commodity export prices remain robust and

are reflected in a rise in our forecast for the
2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price to $9.75/kg. Our
opening bid for the Milk Price in 2027/28 is a
solid $9.50/kg.
• House prices have not stabilised as expected
in recent months. As a result, we have revised
down our 2026 house price forecast from a
small rise to a 1.4% decline.
• The combination of high fuel costs and a weak
housing market will likely crimp consumer
demand running into Christmas.
• We have modestly revised down our GDP

forecasts for the December quarter, which
reduces our 2026 growth forecast to a still
solid 2.5%. Risks appear skewed to the
downside given higher energy prices and long-
term interest rates.
• We will look at our 2027 forecasts in the
coming week.
• Our OCR forecasts remain unchanged. We see
a pause at the October meeting and a hike to
3% at the release of the December Monetary
Policy Statement.
Inflation – All gas, no brakes.
We’ve updated our near-term inflation forecasts in

response to recent global developments.
We continue to estimate that consumer prices rose by
0.7% in the September quarter, consistent with recent
monthly prices updates from Stats NZ. That would see
annual inflation ease to 3.7% in the year to September,
down from 4.1% in the year to June. We’ll confirm that
figure next week, when the final monthly prices update
for the September quarter is released on 16 October.

However, that easing in inflation appears set to be only
a temporary reprieve. We now expect consumer prices
to rise 1.1% in the December quarter. That would see
annual inflation rising to a three-year high of 4.2% by

the end of this year. The main driver of this revision are
sharply higher global fuel prices, compounded by a more
than 4% fall in the New Zealand dollar since the start of
September. Together, those developments have already
pushed local petrol prices up around 50 cents / litre over
the past month, with the nationwide average price of 91
unleaded reaching a record $3.53 / litre earlier this week.

RBNZ target range
Our updated year-end inflation forecast is above the
RBNZ’s September projection of 3.9%. In a recent
presentation, Governor Breman noted that the rise in oil
prices meant the risks to the RBNZ’s near-term inflation
forecast were to the upside. However, she also noted that

the RBNZ’s focus was on the medium-term outlook.
We haven’t changed our quarterly inflation forecasts for
2027 at this stage. However, the upward revision to our
forecast for the December quarter means annual inflation
is set to remain firm through most of 2027, consistent
with the RBNZ’s forecasts. We will review our 2027
forecasts for inflation and growth more comprehensively
next week when we will also review our financial forecasts
with the release of the Westpac Market Outlook.

A more tepid housing market.
We had expected the housing market to stabilise through
the second half of 2026. However, recent trends have
disappointed. House sales volumes continue to weaken,
and the median number of days required to sell a house
has lengthened to 48 days. Not surprisingly, therefore,

average house prices have continued to nudge lower
despite more robust conditions in those areas – mostly
in the South Island – that are most exposed to the well-
performing export sector. The latest indicators suggest
no imminent change in this trend.
Given these weak trends, our previous forecast for
essentially flat house prices through the September and
December quarters now looks too optimistic. We have
revised down our house price forecast for the 2026 year

from a rise of 0.6% y/y to a fall of 1.4% y/y on a national
basis. This is consistent with quarterly falls in house
prices of 0.5% and 0.8% q/q over the September and
December quarters.
The still weak labour market, higher mortgage rates and
still moderate employment growth all point to a weak
housing market for the foreseeable future. Risks remain
tilted towards another weak year in 2027. This would
especially be the case if increased taxation of capital

gains on investor housing were to occur as a result of the
outcome of the 2026 General Election.

It’s likely to be the case that there will be divergent trends
in the housing market across the country. Income and
employment trends in tourism and agriculture dominated

regions are expected to remain robust. Our upgrade to
the 2026/27 Milk Price released in our recent Agri Bites
report emphasises this point. But it looks like it will be
a while before investors and homeowners in the major
metropolitan areas in the North Island will be feeling
wealthier through a rising home value. On balance this
is likely to crimp consumer spending attitudes through
the end of 2026 and might moderate the recovery in the
construction sector that is underway.

Growth forecasts moderated a touch.
Our short-term growth forecasts still appear to be in
the ballpark of what seems likely given recent trends.
Recent consumer and business confidence surveys have

suggested resilience of the economy to recent rises in
energy prices and long-term interest rates. However, it’s
likely the rise in energy prices in particular will eat into
consumer spending appetites towards the end of 2026
should current levels of fuel prices be sustained.
We have knocked off a modest amount off our forecast
for consumer spending in the December quarter to
reflect these risks and will look more closely at the
implications for 2027 growth in coming weeks. Certainly,

the large rise in long term interest rates may be relevant
for 2027 growth trends. The election outcome introduces
two-sided risks to the 2027 growth outlook that are hard
to quantify at this early stage. We currently expect 2.5%
y/y growth for the 2026 year.
We don’t see a need to change our labour market
forecasts for 2026 at this point. Signs continue to
accumulate suggesting strengthening employment
growth – although it will be a while before a noticeable

drop in the unemployment rate is evident.
Implications for the OCR.
We are leaving our forecasts of the OCR unchanged.
Since the July 2026 Monetary Policy Review, we have
anticipated the RBNZ will slow the pace of OCR increases
now the 3% neutral level (in the RBNZ’s view) is coming
into view.
The RBNZ sent a clear signal that they expected to pause
in October and raise the OCR just one more time in
December. We still think this is the most likely scenario.

The recent data has boosted the short-term inflation
profile but at this stage there is little evidence of rising
inflation expectations or more persistent inflationary
pressures. There are some risks to the growth outlook –
albeit two sided – to consider. Taking the time to assess
the impact of the OCR rises to date and the strategy going
forward makes sense to us. We don’t see a shift in the
MPC’s strategy in October.
We continue to think it most likely that the RBNZ will

have more work to do in 2027. However, there is a lot
of water to go under the bridge before then. The OCR
increases that have occurred to date, and the one further
expected increase in December will position the MPC
well to respond to conditions in 2027 as they unfold.
The unexpected rise in long term interest rates that
has occurred in the last 6 weeks or so has tightened
monetary conditions and has at least offset the easing in
conditions coming from the weaker exchange rate over

the same period.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
After posting a soft but better than expected first half, the
Australian economy is facing into more challenges near term
as another round of disruptions from global energy markets
combine with a further rise in interest rates and a deepening

housing downturn. Against this, business investment is in
the midst of a sustained ramp-up led by a large and growing
pipeline of data centre and energy investment projects.
Elsewhere, public demand is growing at a slower pace and
new dwelling investment is likely to moderate but both are
expected to still see positive growth. Overall, the demand
mix should see capacity pressures continue to ease but with
significant risks on either side.
Shifting political winds

Some of these risks are now in the wider policy space. The
political landscape has changed materially since the 2025
Federal Election with opinion polls showing a sustained swing
against the Labor government and a marked rise in support
for One Nation that raises the prospect of a three-horse race
in the next election, the outcome of which could be very hard
to predict (we may get a preview of this with next month’s
Victorian state election). While the next Federal election is

still a long way off – not due until well into 2028 – the shift
does appear to be having some bearing on policy. For example,
the government’s choice not to reinstate the temporary
halving in fuel excise tax that was deployed when fuel prices
first spiked back in March suggests it is less inclined towards
providing broad based cost-of-living support that might make
it harder to bring inflation back down.
Migration policy is shaping more clearly as a battleground.

A suite of changes announced by the government in mid-
September and which came into effect in October, are already
expected to slow working holidaymaker and foreign student
arrivals, moderating total net overseas migration from
around 300k current to 225k by 2028, trimming population
growth from around 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr. For its part, the main
Liberal-National opposition party has proposed to reduce net
migration to 100k in the first two years of government, before

increasing to 130k and 160k in the two years following –
which would likely see total population growth slow to around
0.7-0.8%yr initially. One Nation has proposed a period of ‘net
negative’ migration, lowering net migration by 250k a year
over the first three years of government before considering a
‘ceiling’ of 130k per year thereafter. This would see population
growth slow to around 0.2-0.3%yr initially.
While none of these policies are on the table for this year

or next, the direction of travel is clear with a risk that the
current government moves further towards curbing migrant
More challenges near term ...
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
inflows. Slower population growth basically maps one-to-
one to GDP growth although the specific flow-on impacts to
areas like housing and labour markets are more complex and
will depend on the population mix and how the demand and
supply effects balance out. For the record, we have trimmed

our own population growth forecasts in the 2028 out-year,
from 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr with a related tweak to our GDP
growth forecast for that year.
Consumer sentiment-spending gap
Near term challenges continue to centre on the consumer.
Here, the latest updates show sentiment again plumbing
historical lows but more mixed messages around spending
which is still holding up better than might be expected based
on a simple read on the consumer mood.
Certainly that mood remains deeply negative. The Westpac–

Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index dropped
4.7% to 80.4 in October, putting the month amongst the forty
worst reads since the began in the early 1970s, following
two others reads in this group back in April and June and
another eight recorded in 2022–23. Altogether this marks the
worst period of recurring extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
This has been difficult to square with the soft rather than

outright weak picture on consumer spending through most
of this year. Even when real spending was recording more
material per capita declines back in 2023-24, these were
somewhat milder than what might have been expected given
the weakness in sentiment at the time.
We explored this more fully in our August Red Book . Some
of the disconnect may be a by-product of high inflation which
‘hits different’ sentiment-wise. Cost of living pressures,

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
